which are precisely the ground state energy of the positive asymmetric Little model and its decoupled counterpart in the thermodynamic limit. More on the foundations, importance, relevance, and key state of the art results related to various aspects of Little models can be found in, e.g.,

[
10
,
35
,
6
,
11
,
3
,
61
]
.

Similarly for
s=-1
one has analogously

\lim_{n,\beta\rightarrow\infty}\psi\left(\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}^{n},\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right\}^{n},{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf m},\beta,-1,1\right)=-\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\min_{{\bf x}\in\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\}^{n}}\|G{\bf x}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{nm}}

(257)

and
\lim_{n,\beta\rightarrow\infty}\psi(\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\}^{n},\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\}^{n},{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf m},\beta,-1,0)
as the negative asymmetric Little model ground state energy and its decoupled counterpart in the thermodynamic limit.

5.3
Perceptrons

5.3.1
Spherical perceptron

For
s=-1
,
{\mathcal{X}}={\mathbb{S}}^{n}
, and
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{n}
(where
{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m}
is the positive orthant portion of the
m
-dimensional unit sphere), one has that
\psi(\cdot)
in (
252
) is (a properly adjusted) free energy associated with the classical, so-called, spherical perceptron (see, e.g.,
[
60
,
67
,
66
,
23
,
22
,
51
,
52
]
).
Clearly,
\psi({\mathbb{S}}^{m},{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m},{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf m},\beta,1,0)
is then its intended decoupled comparative counterpart. In random optimizations (see, e.g.,
[
19
,
17
,
18
,
20
,
21
,
2
,
60
,
67
,
66
,
23
,
22
,
49
,
13
,
37
,
77
,
76
,
12
,
29
,
4
,
75
,
51
,
52
]
), one is, again, often interested in

\lim_{n,\beta\rightarrow\infty}\psi\left(\psi({\mathbb{S}}^{m},{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m},{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf m},\beta,1,1\right)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\min_{{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{m}}\max_{{\bf y}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m}}{\bf y}^{T}G{\bf x}}{\sqrt{n}}

(258)

and
\lim_{n,\beta\rightarrow\infty}\psi\left(\psi({\mathbb{S}}^{m},{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m},{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf m},\beta,1,0\right)
which are precisely the spherical perceptron associated ground state energy and its decoupled counterpart in the thermodynamic limit.

5.3.2
Binary perceptron

For
s=-1
,
{\mathcal{X}}=\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\}^{n}
, and
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m}
, one has that
\psi(\cdot)
in (
252
) is (a properly adjusted) free energy associated with the, so-called, binary perceptron (see, e.g.,
[
60
,
23
,
22
,
63
,
32
,
26
,
31
,
74
,
36
,
8
,
48
,
78
,
14
]
).
\psi(\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\}^{n},{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m},{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf m},\beta,1,0)
is then its intended decoupled comparative counterpart. In random optimizations (see, e.g.,
[
60
,
23
,
22
,
63
,
32
,
26
,
31
,
74
,
36
,
8
,
48
,
78
,
14
]
)), one is, again, often interested in

\lim_{n,\beta\rightarrow\infty}\psi\left(\psi(\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}^{n},{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m},{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf m},\beta,1,1\right)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\min_{{\bf x}\in\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right\}^{n}}\max_{{\bf y}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m}}{\bf y}^{T}G{\bf x}}{\sqrt{n}}

(259)

and
\lim_{n,\beta\rightarrow\infty}\psi\left(\psi(\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\}^{n},{\mathbb{S}}_{+}^{m},{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf m},\beta,1,0\right)
which are precisely the spherical perceptron associated ground state energy and its decoupled counterpart in the thermodynamic limit.

The above examples are only a few illustrative ones from a rather unlimited collection (the cited references contain a large number of closely related relevant ones as well). Although small, this set provides a pretty good hint as to how wide could be the range of potential applications of our results. Further studying of the above presented interpolating concepts within the context of each of these applications is therefore of great interest. Such a studying is usually problem specific and we will present many interesting results that can be obtained in these directions in separate papers.

6
Conclusion

A very powerful statistical interpolating/comparison mechanism, to which we refer as
fully lifted
(fl), is presented. The concept is a substantial upgrade of the fully bilinear mechanism introduced in
[
68
]
, which corresponds to the zero level of full or one level of partial lifting. As it contains the mechanism of
[
68
]
as a special case, it also contains all special subcases of
[
68
]
. In particular, the basic well known Slepian’s max and Gordon’s minmax comparison principles are special cases of
[
68
]
and therefore of our work as well. Since many of the results that we created in recent years relied on these well-known principles, it is clear that the generality and magnitude of the results presented here will enable a substantial progress in further studying all of them.

To give a hint regarding the range of potential applications, we showed how one can deduce several classical optimization problems as special cases of our model. These are of course only a few illustrative ones. We should, however, emphasize that the presented mechanism is a very generic and extremely powerful self-sustainable tool which can be used in various extensions as well. Since these are problem specific, we discuss them as well as their final results in separate papers.
